asymmetry. Union the necessary restrictions of every resolved edit path for the same
editor/table/column, including paths without a recovered conditional guard; only
a definite failed implication to that union can emit. Group by creator/table/
column/guard, name the editor and exact guard.
Only after emitting the specific note, suppress the older generic insert-inventory
note for that creator/table/column; failed recovery must retain the generic signal. It cannot
obscure the more specific question; defaults/literals remain quiet. A private
write-only escalation requires a definite failed implication from creator
admission to an explicit column-read gate, not merely an unknown read audience.
The message names that audience and asks whether this caller should initialize
the agency's private field. Both forms remain advisory and never infer the spec.

**Non-null witnesses.** Within the existing finite argument and campaign budgets,
prioritize non-null values for optional arguments directly supplying a declared
column-write intent before the null baseline. Preserve null alternatives and
explicit truncation notices. Only actual admitted writes judged against independent
intent refute; finite failure to find a witness remains unproved. Pin a non-null
042 billing-note witness for an added independent `agencyBillingNotes write only
owner` invariant, with actual GeneratedFixtureTrace/AuthoredFixtureTrace admission.
Its original read/operation intents alone do not assert this missing column policy.
Do not change its runtime rule or fabricate a row.

**Evidence removal.** Add advisory priority-2 `N_EVIDENCE_REMOVAL` for a media child
linked to a parent where the creation operation supplies required media and also
creates the parent with that exact reference bound to the created row id (mere
same-operation co-occurrence is insufficient), or independent parent `always exists child[...]` intent names
that relationship (the existing spelling is pinned by wave22/040-spec.q). Identify authored deletes, changes to the parent-reference
column, and explicit null/empty writes to evidence constrained non-null/nonempty
by independent intent. Group by child/parent-reference and list the media fields
and removing operations. Required media types alone do not prove the parent needs
at least one child; phrase this distinction explicitly. Ask whether the last
required evidence row can be removed, and point to a transactional last-child
guard, replacement, parent retirement, and independent integrity intent plus test.
Ordinary replacements of one non-null image are not removals. An optional
attachment with neither required-creation structure nor independent integrity
intent is outside the detector. A source guard may make removal intentional;
this is reviewable advice, not a gate or a claim that the guard is absent.

Qualification pins the twelve wave-23 loosen lanes 003,007,011,015,019,023,027,
031,035,039,043,047 (including 003's conditional
exception) with zero optional-upload gates; retains explicit parent-required-child and direct non-null intent positive controls;
040 return attachments are a separate negative control; pins a single focused 030 eligibility question,
042's owner-only billing guard and non-null witness, and 028's removal question.
Run corpus measurements before/after, report gate and advice counts separately,
and requalify the packed security guide/starters. Mac verification uses the named
Studio worktree only; SSH unavailability is reported, never counted as a pass.

Day-two fragment/qualification clarifications: these source heuristics are not
proof-completeness analyses. Nonmatching approval synonyms (`verified`, `cleared`,
`vetted`), mixed change conditions and early-return equivalents do not establish
safety and do not generate one uncovered record per syntactic nonmatch. Their
absence is documented here and in the guide; generic inventory and explicit
intent remain available. Pin different-row, mixed-condition, intervening-write
and early-return fallbacks, fee quietness, optional attachment exclusion, unrelated
co-insert exclusion and ordinary replacement controls. Measure all corpus check
latencies against the existing five-second authoring target; no new unbounded
search or larger kernel implication budget is introduced. The same static
creator/column/guard worklist uses the kernel's existing bounded implication.
Stock social qualification now asserts no shipped reviews file, no upload gate
and check exit0 before and after edits. Analyzer sec7-6 rejects sec7-2 through sec7-5 records.

Implementation review repairs: sibling discharge is restricted to a direct ordinary
insert in the mutation's straight-line prefix before any conditional/loop/as/return
control flow, not absence of a branch marker. Conditional expressions, zero-iteration
loops, helpers and if-absent inserts cannot establish that discharge. Stale-check
suppression requires every eligible column on the same checked entity, identified
by its unique key after mapping creator arguments/viewer through immutable stored
fields to the particular later target row or its child reference. A recheck of the
inspector instead of the applicant, another resource, or only one of two columns
cannot suppress. Ambiguous identity retains an advisory question and never suppresses.
Changed-field extraction treats remaining calls/writes in Require and If conditions,
branch requirements and compound expressions as effects; pending facts are discarded
before an effectful condition or use, and cannot cross a write or unknown call.
Fresh helper-local column facts may still be recovered after argument resolution.
These are conservative source-analysis fragments, not a new proof of stability.

Identity normalization is deliberately closed: only literal keys and resolved
immutable created-row captures may establish cross-operation identity. Unmapped
names (`viewer`, arguments), arbitrary reads/calls and other printed expressions
cannot compare equal merely because the same spelling appears in both operations.
A later-read capture field must itself be immutable; sharing an initial value
with a different immutable field does not establish enduring identity. Unknown
creator identity retains the question; unknown decision identity cannot suppress.
The final analyzer revision is sec7-10, invalidating earlier in-progress revisions.

Effect emission also ends pending column facts before evaluating its payload or
once-key, and those expressions are walked for authored writes. Qualification
pins an emit-hidden write fallback, unknown parent-probe filters, exact-parent
mismatch, two independent eligibility groups and two evidence reference groups.
An analyzer upgrade may require existing source-bound reviews to be revisited;
it never silently carries an earlier revision's acknowledgment forward.

Final closed-fragment repairs: named-id selectors must be literal IDs or typed
reference arguments (including `.id` of a read already pinned by those selectors),
never printed mutable lookups. A creator capture is compared only through literal
or immutable operation-input/viewer values, not separately evaluated calls/reads.
A decision that creates/deletes/upserts the captured source table cannot establish
capture stability. Branch/loop assignments poison affected advisory aliases at the
join, including argument shadowing; branch bindings cannot establish a new change
guard by retaining an old environment. Inherited column facts never enter a loop
or survive its domain evaluation. A column guard whose dependencies the editor can
write is not treated as stable. These conservative cuts leave ordinary inventory
and independent replay available, without claiming nonmatching source is safe.

An exact `argument ! else CODE` value enforces non-null at the creating write.
Without independent parent evidence intent this is valid conditional evidence,
not an upload gate (the wave18/19 respirator pass-card controls). With an explicit
parent obligation, an asserted child can discharge it only through the same direct
unconditional-creation fragment; an assertion inside a skipped branch does not
require a child. Omission aborting the atomic mutation cannot commit an evidence-
less parent. Assert-wrapped subexpressions inside a nullable alternative are not
assumed to make the entire field non-null.

To avoid confusing an old captured row with an intervening version, the specific
column class requires exactly one resolved write to that editor's target table,
and literal or immutable operation-input/viewer target keys. Multiple writes or
mutable key expressions keep ordinary inventory. The compiled editor write set
must not intersect recovered authority dependencies. Module-local editors and
creator argument flow remain distinct across source files. Conditional and loop
alias invalidation also applies when the advisory walker expands helper arguments.

Primary-row capture uses compiler-selected read/write-target index metadata cached
by source site and table, never a typed reference passed to a named non-ID lookup.
Explicit non-ID keys stay distinct even when their value has the table's reference
type; unknown or ambiguous selected indexes cannot establish a row-ID capture.
Upload deduplication uses the qualified module/operation, argument, table, column
and source site, and final
diagnostic deduplication preserves source sites, so distinct operations retain
separate individual review IDs even for the same media column.

Final precision boundary: a required media column is already validated at every
executed write; optional argument syntax alone cannot bypass that row contract.
Without independent parent necessity, do not gate an already-protected required
column. Direct nullable argument writes to an optional media column with explicit
non-null intent remain gated; unknown/derived helper returns or nullable expression
alternatives stay in advisory/independent-test coverage, never guessed argument-
omission failures. A non-null collection intent does not require an image when
its declared list allows zero elements. Explicit parent-required-child omission
retains the separate conditional-creation gate, even when the child value itself
is asserted, because a skipped child performs no assertion.

Non-null column-write argument prioritization excludes optional row references and
principals, preserving nullable relationship setup histories such as unclaimed
children. The existing relationship/write-intent regression pins that frontier.

Final precision cuts after independent review: the parent upload gate requires an
actual nullable-argument branch that can skip child creation; an unconditional
child validated through a helper is never guessed to omit evidence. Unknown
helper/control-flow cases remain advisory/testing. Eligibility suppression across
a child-to-parent capture requires exactly one resolved parent identity; multiple
or unknown parent references keep the question. Upload review obligations retain
operation, argument, table, column and source site, including two sites in one
mutation. Analyzer sec7-10 invalidates in-progress revisions2 through9.

The two upload alternatives are independent: a parent-required child skipped on
a nullable upload branch triggers review even when the child media column itself
is nullable. The independent parent invariant requires that child to exist;
omitting the upload skips it. Requiring child media separately applies only to
the direct nullable-column alternative, not to parent existence intent. A repair
may create the required child with deliberate optional media instead of skipping
it. This is a parent-presence obligation, not an inferred non-null child column.

Evidence removal likewise checks independent parent-presence intent before
filtering nullable media. Deleting or detaching that independently required child
remains a grouped advisory question even with nullable photo/file columns.
Parent-presence intent alone does not make a nullable media cell required; its
ordinary clearing is excluded unless independent column non-null intent applies.

Parent omission is relative to the parent creation context: a redundant authored
require inside the skipped child cannot suppress the gate, while a non-null guard
enclosing both parent and child excludes it. The child must have an actual
positive nullable-argument branch. The parent must be a direct straight-line
ordinary insertion whose entire preceding prefix and creation expression do not
inspect that argument. Unknown/helper/conditional parent prefixes stay advisory
and independent replay coverage; do not guess whether they validate the upload.


## 17. SEC8: actionable evidence, honest campaigns and durable decisions

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22. Status: Implemented. This
wave-24 amendment supersedes the name-list restriction in §16.9 and whole-source
review identity in §§16.2/16.5. DX7 owns upgrade/gate messaging and clock/proof UI.

### 17.1 Presence and lifecycle questions

Optional media stays advisory without independent intent. Both the note and the
specification questionnaire offer `invariant TABLE always .COLUMN != null` only
for permanent integrity, and `invariant TABLE.insert only .COLUMN != null` for
creation-only presence (later redaction remains possible). The upload gate also
recognizes positive non-null conjuncts in independent insert/write intent for
that exact column; OR and unknown predicates cannot establish this gate.
The upload recipe begins with that decision. Never compile the questionnaire or
infer mandatory evidence from natural language. Existing explicit-intent gates
remain the only gates. A parent with an independently attached media child gets
an advisory when a literal enum transition lacks a dominating child existence
requirement; text photo/URL fields are explicitly labelled name-based questions.

Stale eligibility considers any literal enum update distinct from the creation
state, without interpreting words such as filled/confirmed. It still requires
mutable eligibility on the same resource and keeps the exact recheck precision
of §16.9. Approval questions cover approval-named flags ignored by lifecycle
writes, and fields checked by an authorized decision but editable without that
decision's lifecycle restrictions. Evidence replacement after approval is included.
Time questions cover overdue/missed states without a server-clock check,
deadlines bounded against now where a dependent stored deadline exists, and
365-day multiples used as anniversaries. A guard-state question asks whether a
boolean requirement should be consumed by a transition. All these are bounded
structural/name-based questions, never proof, inferred intent or new gates.

### 17.2 Campaign honesty and useful generated roots

Every invariant outside the finite evaluator's fragment is named by target and
source site in default test output with its reason; a separate evaluation-gap
count prevents zero violations being read as full invariant coverage. Static
unproved remains unproved. No unknown evaluation becomes policy false. Inherited
raw read-through child assertions remain explicit evaluation gaps; this amendment
does not add raw evaluator capability. Future support requires §8.1 seed ingress,
committed readback, parent lifetime/retirement and final-exists validation. A
snapshot-only comparison never sets refuted.
Generated required uploads use real verified bytes and actor-owned fresh ledger
entries, satisfying declared byte limits; no argument/type/admission bypass.
Generated creators reserve at most two rows per target table. After an admitted
creator tuple, try a tuple differing in at least one boolean before another actor
can consume the second row slot; this is not complete Cartesian coverage. Keep
the existing 16 tuple/2048 creator-call initialization limits and report omitted
boolean combinations. Directed exploration uses depth 12, 4096 aggregate attempts,
eight argument tuples per operation/world, three distinct successors per operation,
and a five-world beam (at most 16 retained worlds including current/next/successors).
These bounds are shared, not multiplied per creator boolean. Breadth remains 512.
Check retains its shared 512-work/1500ms cap; no new cap confers proof. Report every
cut and retain generated provenance.

### 17.3 Presentation and review identity

Human output collapses restatement and role-wide questions into one questionnaire
per table. Full original diagnostics, audiences and source sites remain available
in JSON and verbose audit. The generated draft remains the complete questionnaire.
Advisories accept the same per-note rationale/disposition command as gates and
print a discoverable route to it; no bulk acknowledgment and no proof promotion.

A review ID binds analyzer revision, diagnostic code, subject and stable semantic
content, excluding line/column locations. Its source binding is a semantic slice:
the relevant rule/column and its dependency closure, or conservatively the source
declaration and referenced/writing declarations for complex flow notes. Comments,
line shifts and independent declarations do not invalidate it. Changing a relevant
rule/column, helper, grant writer, role mutator or independent intent does.
Conservative fallback scope must be explicit and tested; never silently use an
empty dependency set. Existing version-1 records remain readable but become stale
once at analyzer revision change. Keep source-race checks, lock, atomic rename,
invalid-file refusal and no-writing-on-refutation semantics unchanged. Dispositions
remain source-control review records, never application authority.

### 17.4 Qualification

Positive and suppression regressions cover each detector and generated upload
roots, evaluation gaps, boolean alternatives, durable advisory/gate dispositions
and precise invalidation. Re-measure copies of all thirteen non-judgment wave-24
findings in three modes (no intent, authored intent, one independent spec line),
separating advice, gates and reachable refutations. Report corpus gate counts and
inspect every new gate across 450 projects. Run the commissioned full verification
and packed-guide checks; do not claim an unavailable check passed.

### 17.5 Review resolutions and exact finite fragments

Each original advisory remains exit-0/display-only and independently addressable
by its JSON securityReviews ID. A collapsed N_TABLE_QUESTIONNAIRE has no review ID;
it is only a human projection. Verbose/JSON retain underlying notes. No change to
refutation, lock, atomic publication or source-race refusal semantics.

Analyzer is `sec8-1`. IDs hash JSON(analyzer, code, source-declaration identity,
normalized diagnostic message, repair), then JSON(base hash, occurrence ordinal).
Declaration identity is schema-relative file, declaration kind and name. Message
normalization removes only numeric source line/column suffixes; literal values,
operation/table/column names, and predicate text remain. Rewording may invalidate
reviews conservatively; this is deliberate. Identical repeated diagnostics get
individual ordinal IDs in deterministic source order; changing their declaration
changes the shared source binding, so removal/reordering cannot transfer an old
acknowledgment across changed write contexts. Tests retain one pending review
after acknowledging only one repeat, including after line shifts.

Bindings are pure source/compiled-schema functions:
- Scalar optional-evidence column notes bind that column's lexer tokens, excluding
  locations/comments and unrelated columns. Complex optional-evidence flows fall
  through to the declaration closure.
- Restatement and role/broad-read notes bind the effective row/column rule, exact
  column type, matching independent intent, transitive dependency schemas and all
  bound programs writing those dependencies. Non-read intent includes write rules
  and field ownership. Source sites are stripped before canonical serialization.
- All other codes, including both gates, bind the containing declaration and every
  declaration named in the note; transitively include referenced declarations,
  all mutations/jobs that write a selected table, and its independent invariants.
  Helper and sibling-write bodies are therefore included. Canonical lexer tokens
  retain values and indentation but exclude comments, locations and newlines.
  This deliberately conservative closure may invalidate another note on the same
  affected resource; it never binds an independent declaration merely for sharing
  a source file. Unlocated unknown notes use an explicitly labelled whole-source
  fallback. Never bind an empty semantic slice. Old analyzer records stay readable
  but cannot acknowledge this analyzer's notes.

The advisory detectors are intentionally finite and may ask unnecessary questions:
- stale eligibility retains §16.9's same-resource/column identity extraction but
  admits any literal mutable enum destination other than its default creation
  value, including rejection/inspection; true approval flags retain the existing
  `approv`/`authoriz` substrings. No claim that every destination needs rechecking.
- approval-not-enforced: a true `approv`/`authoriz` Boolean is authored, and a
  same-table enum updater does not inspect it in its dominating resolved requires.
- edit-after-approval: a media column on an approval/stage-bearing table, or a
  payload inspected by another literal-enum/approval-flag decision, is edited
  without positive equality to the same target's default creation state. A
  negative cancelled-state check does not prove editing precedes approval. This
  does not claim arbitrary approvals or alternative enforcement are unsupported.
- child presence: an authored media-child creator plus a direct parent reference
  and a parent enum updater, without a positive dominating `exists child` conjunct
  keyed to that exact parent reference/target. Negation, another parent and late
  requires do not suppress. Text field substrings photo/image/scan/sketch/url are
  explicitly name-based. This asks for spec intent, never implies a gate.
- missed/overdue/expired literal destinations ask about server-clock enforcement;
  dependent windows compare written Time arguments bounded against now with
  uninspected referenced Time columns named due/deadline/until/expire. Calendar
  questions recognize integer `Nd` literals divisible by 365, N >= 365. They make
  no calendar inference. Unsupported affine forms remain outside these heuristics.
- reusable guards: a dominating Boolean/enum check on a related table, or Boolean
  check on the transitioned table, is not assigned by that operation. This is a
  question about reuse, not an assertion that every guard must be consumed.

Qualification lanes/findings: 007 F2; 008 F1; 011 F1; 013 F1; 015 F1/F2;
016 F1; 029 F1; 031 F1; 033 F1; 036 F1/F2; 048 F1. Frozen source hashes,
three-mode outcomes and the exact corpus manifest belong to the SEC8 report;
no generic claim that advice has prevented an exploit. Gate counts are separated
from violations; every newly gated original project requires individual inspection.

Second-review corrections: reverse writer dependencies use compiled IR, including
helper-expanded writes; grouped column advice binds every represented column,
not only its lead column. Concrete evaluation reports contextual principal/string
coercion gaps and records every visited policy-unknown result with target/site.
Creator initialization reports omitted boolean projections at tuple/population
cuts. Only a status/stage/state enum or approval/authorization Boolean default can suppress
edit-after-approval advice; an unrelated default-valued field cannot. Overdue-clock
suppression requires an ordered server-now comparison on the transitioned row.

Third-review corrections: edit suppression recognizes only default equality on
`status`, `stage`, `state`, or an `approv`/`authoriz` Boolean; arbitrary enums and
Booleans cannot stand in for workflow approval. Unknown lifecycle spellings stay
advisory. Generated initialization checks every target's committed candidate row
count before retaining it: a candidate exceeding two rows on any target is declined
with an explicit coverage gap, even for mixed populated/empty targets or a
multi-insert creator. This is exploration pruning, never runtime admission.

### 17.6 Measurement-driven history corrections

The first 13-finding matrix exposed two lost histories: child creation populated
all evidence before observing parent-only roots (008), and nullable approval times
were absent from state signatures (036 F2). Observe the existing validated root
before the first creator taking a reference to an already populated authored
resource, as well as before update/upsert creators. Keep the existing single
pre-derived observation point and shared attempt budgets; no new initial-world
proof or application-reachability claim is introduced.

Directed signatures retain Time values and optional presence, in addition to
status/role/Boolean/relationship state. Optional media/text values contribute only
null/non-null, avoiding upload-ID noise. Time/presence changes receive three
priority points so an approval-only commit can survive to its dependent transition;
this is exploration ordering, never a semantic quotient or proof. Keep old payload
merge coverage warnings. The beam is five, not six: transient successor insertion
plus an optional old-image fork must stay inside the 16-world envelope. Reported
peak counters remain conservative; do not hide a transient allocation by moving
the counter after truncation. Regressions require witnesses for the exact appended
specification invariant, not merely a nonzero count on some other invariant.

The next actual replay exposed the final 036 F2 blocker: a creator's default +1d
event date can never pass a decision requiring 14d lead time. Time arguments now
sample source-derived Add/Sub integer offsets of at least one day from the bound
programs (candidates, never type/proof assumptions). Cache at most 64 distinct
offsets per campaign. Try the furthest future boundary first, keep the existing
+1d/now/-1d controls, then offset boundaries ±1ms on both sides of now; retain at
most sixteen distinct samples and name every cut. Checked arithmetic declines
overflow. Existing 16 creator tuples, 2048 initialization calls, two-row targets,
4096 directed attempts and 512 general argument/breadth bounds remain unchanged.
No lane, operation name or specification literal receives an exception.

### 17.7 Preserve past-time histories alongside future setup

The full affected suite exposed a regression in the new future-first Time
ordering: directed argument grouping retained only one Time value per identity
tuple. That could erase the previously covered past-deadline creator before a
query on an unrelated reference. Keep the furthest future boundary first for
lead-time setup, then the past-day control before future-day/now controls.
Directed grouping retains original Time alternatives (also nullable Time) in
addition to Boolean/enum alternatives; all existing eight-tuple, three-successor,
five-world, 4096-attempt and 16-world bounds remain. This preserves candidates,
not Cartesian completeness or proof. Existing omission messages remain truthful.
The existing wrong-reference deadline regression must pass with an actual
one-step creator trace containing a strictly past timestamp, while the SEC8
14-day approval and exact-invariant-site regressions continue to pass.

### 17.8 Past-time breadth controls

The exact runtime regression still failed after r7 because it uses breadth
histories, not directed exploration. Its first future-deadline trace records a
paired oracle witness under the existing compact operation/viewer key, which can
then displace a later past-deadline direct payload. Preserve the old search
control: in the breadth mutator candidate plan, stably front-load cases with at
least one Time (or nullable Time) argument strictly before the campaign clock.
Generated initialization and directed exploration retain future-first ordering
and their source-derived lead-time candidates. Keep the existing compact witness
key, duplicate suppression and append-only witness population unchanged. All
attempt, observer and world bounds remain; this ordering is not complete coverage.
The existing wrong-reference deadline regression must retain its strictly past,
one-step creator payload witness; the 14-day generated approval must also pass.

R8 proposed replacing paired witnesses with direct payloads. Its Codex review
found that removing prior entries invalidates length-based generated-origin
relabeling. That proposal was withdrawn before landing; the r8 snapshot and both
verbatim reviews remain in the review record. No witness replacement, origin
relabeling change or provenance relaxation is part of the final implementation.

### 17.9 Preserve the pre-transition root when observing dependent children

The complete language suite exposed that §17.6's first related-creator trigger
could spend the single pre-derived observation before a resource creator taking
an unrelated reference (for example, an application taking a truck). That lost
the previously covered create/inspect/suspend/approve history. Narrow the early
related-creator trigger: an existing referenced parent must itself be an invariant
target, and that invariant's bound rule policy must depend on a table this
creator inserts/upserts. This observes the parent before its required child,
without preempting the existing first update/upsert/delete-creator observation
for unrelated resource setup. Preserve the single observation point, validated
generated origin and all shared budgets; no new worlds or inferred intent.
Predicates outside bound rule-policy extraction retain their explicit evaluation
gaps and do not gain an inferred dependency. Existing wave22 pickup/suspension
and wave24 child-photo regressions must both replay the exact declared invariant.

### 17.10 Concrete-intent child dependencies

The wave24 child-photo regression uses a concrete assertion with named equality
keys whose predicate has no extracted RuleExpr policy. Rule-policy-only child
dependencies in §17.9 therefore missed it. For search ordering, union each
invariant target's bound-policy table dependencies with explicit Read, Exists and
Created table names found by the existing AST visitor in its retained syntax.
Compute this map once per generated fixture. Keep the exact parent-table match
and creator insert/upsert-target match; arbitrary names/string literals do not
create dependencies. This ordering never establishes evaluator support: clock,
read-through and other unsupported fragments keep all their evaluation gaps.
The one pre-derived observation, all attempt/world caps, ordinary admission,
append-only witnesses and generated-origin relabeling remain unchanged. Both
the legacy pending-root suspension and concrete child-presence regressions must
pass at their exact invariant sites, alongside the existing approval-Time case.
This extends §17.9's dependency recognition; it does not infer a requirement from
an unrelated reference or add invariant evaluation capability.

### DX9 review record — 2026-10-24

The four DX9 amendments were reviewed together. Revision 1: Codex READY,
Muse NOT READY (identity/atomicity, inference, deadline and signal details).
Revision 2: both NOT READY (clock polarity/identity and invalidation precision).
Revision 3: both READY on the same text. The author commissioned autonomous
implementation and extra review rounds in the DX9 workstream brief.

Verbatim reviews and exact revision-3 text/provenance:
[brief](./reviews/3000.000.000.001-dx9-r3-brief.md),
[Muse](./reviews/3000.000.000.001-dx9-r3-muse.txt),
[fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.001-dx9-r3-codex.txt),
[provenance](./reviews/3000.000.000.001-dx9-r3-provenance.json).

 succeeded in 0ms:
//! Concrete question evidence; this never changes gate or invariant verdicts.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#19-wave-28-output-and-witness-decisions-dx11 — inferred witnesses never become authored refutations.
use super::*;
use snapback4_lang::{Diagnostic,security::challenges::{self,Counterexample}};
use snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType;
fn hash(bytes:&[u8])->String {use sha2::{Digest,Sha256};format!("{:x}",Sha256::digest(bytes))}
// Generated values only: retain typed scalars, redact opaque payloads.
fn safe(value:&snapback4_core::Value,kind:Option<&ColumnType>)->serde_json::Value {
    use snapback4_core::Value;
    match (value,kind.map(ColumnType::required)) {
        (_,Some(ColumnType::Text{..}|ColumnType::Json{..}|ColumnType::Bytes{..}|ColumnType::Image{..}|ColumnType::File{..}|ColumnType::Video{..}))=>json!("<redacted>"),
        (Value::Array(xs),Some(ColumnType::List{of,..}|ColumnType::ListRange{of,..}))=>json!(xs.iter().map(|v|safe(v,Some(of))).collect::<Vec<_>>()),
        (Value::String(_)|Value::Bytes(_)|Value::Row(_)|Value::Map(_)|Value::Array(_),None)=>json!("<redacted>"),
        _=>serde_json::to_value(value).unwrap(),
    }
}
fn image(bound:&Bound,table:&str,row:&snapback4_core::Value)->serde_json::Value {
    let snapback4_core::Value::Row(fields)=row else{return json!("<redacted>");};
    json!(fields.iter().map(|(name,value)|(name,safe(value,bound.schema.table(table).and_then(|t|t.columns.get(name))))).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>())
}
fn actual_args(c:&Counterexample)->&BTreeMap<String,snapback4_core::Value> {
    if !c.args.is_empty(){return &c.args;}
    c.history.iter().rev().find(|step|step.operation==c.operation&&step.viewer==c.viewer).map(|step|&step.args).unwrap_or(&c.args)
}
fn evidence(bound:&Bound,inv:&security::Invariant,c:&Counterexample)->serde_json::Value {
    let args=actual_args(c).iter().map(|(name,value)|(name,safe(value,None))).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>();
    let old=c.old.as_ref().map(|row|image(bound,&inv.table,row));let next=image(bound,&inv.table,&c.row);
    let summary=format!("{}({}) as {} → ok; {} {} -> {}; candidate invariant {} only {} (finite generated-fixture replay, not proof)",c.operation,serde_json::to_string(&args).unwrap(),c.viewer,inv.target_label(),old.as_ref().map(|v|v.to_string()).unwrap_or_else(||"absent".into()),next,inv.target,inv.predicate);
    let history=c.history.iter().map(|step|json!({"operation":step.operation,"viewer":step.viewer,"args":step.args.iter().map(|(k,v)|(k,safe(v,None))).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>(),"state":step.result["state"]})).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let supporting=c.supporting_rows.iter().map(|(table,rows)|(table,rows.iter().map(|r|image(bound,table,r)).collect::<Vec<_>>())).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>();
    json!({"history":history,"supportingRows":supporting,"summary":summary,"candidate":format!("invariant {} only {}",inv.target_label(),inv.predicate),"operation":c.operation,"viewer":c.viewer,"args":args,"effect":c.effect,"old":old,"row":next,"reachability":c.reachability,"scope":c.scope})
}
fn candidate(sources:&[(String,String)],line:&str)->Option<security::Invariant> {
    let mut sources=sources.to_vec();sources.push(("$dx11-question.q".into(),format!("{line}\n")));
    let bound=snapback4_lang::compile(&sources);if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty(){return None;}
    let mut inv=bound.invariants.into_iter().find(|i|i.site.file=="$dx11-question.q")?;
    inv.site.file=format!("$derived/dx11/{}",hash(line.as_bytes()));Some(inv)
}
fn candidates(bound:&Bound,sources:&[(String,String)],notes:&[Diagnostic])->Vec<(usize,security::Invariant)> {
    let derived=security::derived::invariants(sources,bound);let mut out=Vec::new();
    for (index,n) in notes.iter().enumerate() {
        if n.code=="N_DERIVED_WRITE_AUTHORITY" {for inv in &derived {if inv.site==n.site {out.push((index,inv.clone()));}}}
        if n.code=="N_INSERT_WRITE_INTENT" {
            if let Some(subject)=n.message.split(": ").nth(1).and_then(|s|s.split_whitespace().next()) {
                for inv in &derived {if subject==format!("{}.{}",inv.table,inv.column.as_deref().unwrap_or("")) {out.push((index,inv.clone()));}}
                if let Some(policy)=n.message.split("without the edit restriction of ").nth(1).and_then(|s|s.split_once(": ").map(|(_,p)|p)).and_then(|s|s.split("; creation admission:").next()) {
                    if let Some(inv)=candidate(sources,&format!("invariant {subject} update only {policy}")) {out.push((index,inv));}
                }
            }
        }
        if n.code=="N_ARGUMENT_GRANT" {
            let Some(subject)=n.message.split("reader-grant column ").nth(1) else{continue;};let Some((table,column))=subject.split_once('.') else{continue;};
            // Name-matched role membership is a candidate question, never a rule.
            for (roles,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables() {for (role,kind) in &t.columns {
                if let ColumnType::Enum{variants}=kind.required() {if variants.iter().any(|v|v==column) {
                    if let Some(principal)=t.columns.iter().find_map(|(c,k)|matches!(k.required(),ColumnType::Principal).then_some(c)) {
                        if let Some(inv)=candidate(sources,&format!("invariant {table} always exists {roles}[{principal} = .{column}, {role} = '{column}']")) {out.push((index,inv));}
                    }
                }}
            }}
        }
        if n.code=="N_APPROVAL_NOT_ENFORCED" {
            let Some((subject,_))=n.message.split_once(':') else{continue;};let Some((table,column))=subject.split_once('.') else{continue;};let Some(t)=bound.schema.table(table) else{continue;};
            if !matches!(t.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required),Some(ColumnType::Bool)){continue;}
            let stem=column.to_lowercase().split("approv").next().unwrap_or("").to_owned();
            let trigger=t.columns.iter().find_map(|(c,k)|(c!=column&&!stem.is_empty()&&c.to_lowercase().starts_with(&stem)&&matches!(k.required(),ColumnType::Bool)).then_some(c));
            let predicate=trigger.map(|trigger|format!("not .{trigger} or .{column}")).unwrap_or_else(||format!(".{column}"));
            let Some(ops)=n.message.split("transitions ").nth(1).and_then(|s|s.split(" do not").next()) else{continue;};
            // A column-change candidate uses the campaign's mature transition
            // exploration; it observes the same operation, without adding a rule.
            fn columns(value:&serde_json::Value,table:&str,out:&mut std::collections::BTreeSet<String>) {
                if let Some(write)=value.get("WriteTarget").filter(|w|w["target"]["table"]==table) {
                    if let Some(fields)=write["patch"]["Object"].as_object(){out.extend(fields.keys().cloned());}
                }
                if let Some(write)=value.get("Update").filter(|w|w["table"]==table) {
                    if let Some(fields)=write["patch"]["Object"].as_object(){out.extend(fields.keys().cloned());}
                }
                match value {serde_json::Value::Object(xs)=>for v in xs.values(){columns(v,table,out)},serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>for v in xs{columns(v,table,out)},_=>{}}
            }
            for op in ops.split(", ").collect::<Vec<_>>().into_iter().rev() {
                if let Some(program)=bound.programs.iter().find(|p|p.program.name==op) {
                    let mut changed=std::collections::BTreeSet::new();columns(&serde_json::to_value(&program.program).unwrap(),table,&mut changed);
                    for col in changed.into_iter().filter(|c|matches!(t.columns.get(c).map(ColumnType::required),Some(ColumnType::Time))) {
                        if let Some(inv)=candidate(sources,&format!("invariant {table}.{col} update only {predicate}")) {out.push((index,inv));}
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }out
}
/// Once an edit is admitted, try a concrete decreasing edit through that same
/// operation. Both writes run on the engine; no fabricated old/new values.
fn decreasing_edit(bound:&Bound,inv:&security::Invariant,c:&Counterexample)->Option<Counterexample> {
    use snapback4_core::{Engine,Context,Value};
    let column=inv.column.as_ref()?;
    if !matches!(bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].columns.get(column)?.required(),ColumnType::Money{..}|ColumnType::Int|ColumnType::IntRange{..}|ColumnType::Decimal{..}) || !actual_args(c).contains_key(column) {return None;}
    let mut engine=Engine::memory(bound.schema.clone(),bound.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone())).ok()?;
    if inv.policy.as_ref().is_some_and(|p|p.any_term(&|term|matches!(term,snapback4_core::schema::RuleTerm::Field(c)|snapback4_core::schema::RuleTerm::OldField(c)|snapback4_core::schema::RuleTerm::NextField(c) if c==column))){return None;}
    let mut dependencies=inv.policy.as_ref().map(security::kernel::dependencies).unwrap_or_default();dependencies.insert(inv.table.clone());
    if bound.programs.iter().find(|p|p.program.name==c.operation).is_some_and(|p|snapback4_core::ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(table,_)|table!=&inv.table&&dependencies.contains(table))){return None;}
    let id=c.old.as_ref()?.field("id").ok()?.clone();
    let mut rows=c.supporting_rows.iter().filter(|(table,_)|dependencies.contains(*table)).flat_map(|(table,rows)|rows.iter().map(move |row|(table.clone(),row.clone()))).filter(|(table,row)|table!=&inv.table||row.field("id").ok()!=Some(&id)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    rows.push((inv.table.clone(),c.old.clone()?));engine.seed_at(rows,challenges::NOW).ok()?;
    let mut high=actual_args(c).clone();high.insert(column.clone(),Value::Int(54321));
    let first=engine.mutate(&c.operation,None,Context::new(&c.viewer,high.clone()).at(challenges::NOW)).ok()?;
    if serde_json::to_value(&first).ok()?["state"]!="sent"{return None;}
    let old=engine.store().rows(&inv.table).ok()?.into_iter().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==Some(&id))?;
    let mut low=actual_args(c).clone();low.insert(column.clone(),Value::Int(0));
    let second=engine.mutate(&c.operation,None,Context::new(&c.viewer,low.clone()).at(challenges::NOW)).ok()?;
    if serde_json::to_value(&second).ok()?["state"]!="sent"{return None;}
    let row=engine.store().rows(&inv.table).ok()?.into_iter().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==Some(&id))?;
    if !matches!((old.field(column).ok()?,row.field(column).ok()?),(Value::Int(a),Value::Int(b)) if a>b){return None;}
    let mut next=c.clone();next.args=low.clone();next.old=Some(old);next.row=row;
    if let Some(step)=c.history.last() {let mut first_step=step.clone();first_step.operation=c.operation.clone();first_step.viewer=c.viewer.clone();first_step.args=high;first_step.result=serde_json::to_value(first).ok()?;next.history.push(first_step);let mut second_step=step.clone();second_step.operation=c.operation.clone();second_step.viewer=c.viewer.clone();second_step.args=low;second_step.result=serde_json::to_value(second).ok()?;next.history.push(second_step);}
    Some(next)
}
/// Fixed work cap; generated cache never clears a gate or supplies authority.
pub(in crate::verbs) fn collect(project:&Project,bound:&Bound,sources:&[(String,String)],notes:&[Diagnostic],expanded:bool)->Vec<Option<serde_json::Value>> {
    let key=hash(&serde_json::to_vec(&("dx11-witness-5",sources,notes,record::capture(sources,None).ok())).unwrap());let path=project.schema_dir().join("generated/security-witnesses.json");
    if let Some(cached)=std::fs::read(&path).ok().and_then(|b|serde_json::from_slice::<serde_json::Value>(&b).ok()) {if cached["key"]==key {if let Ok(values)=serde_json::from_value(cached["values"].clone()){return values;}}}
    if !expanded {return vec![None;notes.len()];}
    let selected=candidates(bound,sources,notes);let mut result=vec![None;notes.len()];
    if !selected.is_empty() {
        let mut questions=bound.clone();questions.invariants=selected.iter().map(|(_,i)|i.clone()).collect();
        let mut campaign=challenges::Campaign{work_remaining:Some(8192),..Default::default()};challenges::generated_campaign(&questions,&mut campaign,false);
        for (index,inv) in &selected {
            if result[*index].is_some(){continue;}
            if let Some(c)=campaign.violations.iter().filter(|c|c.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()&&c.reachability.is_reachable()).max_by_key(|c| {
                let decrease=inv.column.as_ref().and_then(|column|Some((c.old.as_ref()?.field(column).ok()?,c.row.field(column).ok()?))).is_some_and(|(old,next)|matches!((old,next),(snapback4_core::Value::Int(a),snapback4_core::Value::Int(b)) if a>b));
                (decrease,c.history.len())
            }) {let replay=decreasing_edit(bound,inv,c);result[*index]=Some(evidence(bound,inv,replay.as_ref().unwrap_or(c)));}
        }
    }
    let temporary=path.with_extension(format!("{}.tmp",std::process::id()));
    if std::fs::write(&temporary,serde_json::to_vec(&json!({"key":key,"values":result})).unwrap()).is_ok(){let _=std::fs::rename(&temporary,&path);}let _=std::fs::remove_file(temporary);result
}
#[cfg(test)]mod tests {
 use super::*;
 #[test]fn opaque_argument_image_payloads_are_redacted(){
  let secret=snapback4_core::Value::String("not-a-real-credential-sentinel".into());
  for kind in [None,Some(ColumnType::Text{max:100}),Some(ColumnType::Json{max_bytes:100})] {assert!(!safe(&secret,kind.as_ref()).to_string().contains("credential-sentinel"));}
  assert_eq!(safe(&snapback4_core::Value::Int(42),None),json!({"Int":42}));
 }
}
    if !matches!(note.code.as_str(), "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" | "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE") { return None; }
    // @ref LLP 3000.013.002#3 — the checked source repair leads, followed by impact and audience.
    let subject = note.message.split(": ").next().unwrap_or("query");
    let action = if let Some((_, code)) = crate::note_rank::compiled_form(&note.code, &note.rewrite) {
        format!("rewrite for {subject}:\n{}", code.lines().map(|line| format!("    {line}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n"))
    } else if note.rewrite.contains("no verified complete form for this read") {
        format!("review {subject}: analyzer limitation; no verified complete rewrite; keep the query and handle partial data (check --verbose explains why)")
    } else if note.rewrite.starts_with("add `cap ") {
        format!("rewrite for {subject}: {}", note.rewrite)
    } else if let Some(columns) = note.message.split("` reads ").nth(1).and_then(|tail| tail.split_once(", which the scan does not pin").map(|(columns,_)| columns)) {
        format!("review {subject}: missing pin {columns}; choose the intended scope without dropping other readers (check --verbose)")
    } else {
        format!("review {subject}: no checked rewrite; keep the query and handle partial data (check --verbose)")
    };
    let admitted = ["for callers `", "except for callers `"].into_iter().find_map(|needle| {
        note.message.split_once(needle)?.1.split_once("` admits").map(|(rule, _)| rule)
    });
    let mut who = match admitted {
        Some(rule) => format!("audience: `{rule}` is proved complete; other callers may be capped"),
        None if note.code == "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE" => "audience: pinned parent readers are proved complete; other callers may be capped".into(),
        None => "audience: completeness is not proved for every caller; runtime complete may still be true".into(),
    };
    if let Some(scan) = note.message.split_once(" over ").and_then(|(_, tail)| tail.split_once(" is ").map(|(scan, _)| scan)) {
        who.push_str(&format!(" (scan {scan})"));
    }
    Some([action, "impact: fine for a screen showing returned rows; proving absence or all matches needs complete: true; keep partial data visible".into(), who])
}

/// `check --emit-lean <file.q>`: one file, checked, as the Lean model's
/// schema, helpers and programs (`snapback4_lang::emit::lean`).
// @ref LLP 3000.035#52-stage-2-a-small-lean-core — the emitter's command-line face; the harness calls the library directly
fn emit_lean(file: &str) -> i32 {
    let source = match std::fs::read_to_string(file) {
        Ok(source) => source,
        Err(error) => { eprintln!("cannot read {file}: {error}"); return 2; }
    };
    let name = Path::new(file).file_name().and_then(|n| n.to_str()).unwrap_or(file).to_owned();
    match snapback4_lang::emit::lean::lean(&[(name, source)]) {
        Ok(module) => { println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&module.to_json()).unwrap()); 0 }
        Err(snapback4_lang::emit::lean::LeanError::Diagnostics(problems)) => {
            for problem in problems { eprintln!("{problem}"); }
            1
        }
        Err(snapback4_lang::emit::lean::LeanError::Unsupported(u)) => {
            eprintln!("outside the Lean model's fragment: {u}");
            1
        }
    }
}

pub fn check(options: &Options) -> i32 {
    if options.flags.contains_key("audit") { return security::audit(options); }
    if options.flags.contains_key("store") && options.flags.contains_key("release") {
        return releases::check_release(options);
    }

    if let Some(file) = options.flags.get("emit-lean") {
        return emit_lean(file);
    }
    let project = project_or_exit();
    let started = Instant::now();
    package_versions::warn(&project);
    upgrade_history::report(&project, options.json);
    if options.flags.contains_key("fix") {
        if let Err(code) = fix::checked(fix::compile(&project)) { return code; }
    }
    let compiled_sources = match project.sources() {
        Ok(sources) => sources,
        Err(error) => { eprintln!("cannot read snapback/: {error}"); return 2; }
    };
    let mut bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&compiled_sources);
    let reviewable_notes = bound.notes.iter().map(note_reviews::fingerprint).collect();
    for warning in saved_fixture_drift::warnings(&project) {
        if warning.drift { eprintln!("check: WARNING: saved fixture drift: {}", warning.message); }
        else { eprintln!("check: info: {}", warning.message); }
    }
    if options.flags.contains_key("fix") && bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
        if let Err(error) = fix_fixtures::run(&project, &bound, options) { failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_INPUT", format!("check --fix: {error}")); return 2; }
    }

    if let Some(hint) = project.saved_copy_hint() { if options.json { eprintln!("check: {hint}"); } else { println!("check: {hint}"); } }
    if let Some(error) = crate::exact_device::inspect(&project.root, &bound.schema).into_iter().find_map(Result::err) {
        if options.json { println!("{}", json!({"ok": false, "denied": {"code": "E_DEVICE_VERSION", "message": error}, "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": bound.notes, "acknowledgedNotes": []})); }
        else { eprintln!("{error}"); }
        return 1;
    }
    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
        check_surface::report(&project, &bound, options.json, None);
        report_diagnostics(&bound, options.json, true, options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
        if !options.json { print_prediction(&bound, options.flags.contains_key("verbose")); }
        return 1;
    }
    match rollback_views::check(&project,&compiled_sources,&bound,options.flags.get("store")) {
        Ok(diagnostics)=>bound.diagnostics.extend(diagnostics),
        Err(error)=>{failed_check(&bound,options.json,"E_ADOPT",error);return 1;}
    }
    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty(){report_diagnostics(&bound,options.json,true,options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));return 1;}
    let security = security::report(&project, &bound, &compiled_sources, options);
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.3 — check judges adoption against the dev store's active generation and rows, read-only
    match adoption::diagnostics(&project, &bound.schema, options.flags.get("store"), options.json || options.flags.contains_key("verbose")) {
        Ok((problems, notes)) => { bound.diagnostics.extend(problems); bound.notes.extend(notes); },
        Err(error) => { failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_STORE", format!("cannot check store adoption: {error}")); return 2; }
    }
    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
        check_surface::report(&project, &bound, options.json, None);
        report_diagnostics(&bound, options.json, true, options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
        if !options.json { print_prediction(&bound, options.flags.contains_key("verbose")); }
        return 1;
    }
    for diagnostic in readiness::notes(&project, &bound, options.flags.get("fixture")) {
        if diagnostic.code == "E_FIXTURE_TIME" { bound.diagnostics.push(diagnostic); }
        else { bound.notes.push(diagnostic); }
    }
    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
        report_diagnostics(&bound, options.json, true, options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
        return 1;
    }
    bound.notes.extend(snapback4_lang::bind::residency::change_notes(&bound, &residency::previous(&project)));
    if let Err(error) = project.write_generated(&bound) {
        failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_STORE", format!("cannot write generated types: {error}"));
        return 2;
    }
    if let Err(error) = project.check_server() {
        if options.json { println!("{}", json!({ "ok": false, "problems": [{ "code": if error.starts_with("E_SERVER_TOOLCHAIN") { "E_SERVER_TOOLCHAIN" } else { "E_SERVER_TYPECHECK" }, "message": error, "guide": "actions" }], "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": bound.notes, "acknowledgedNotes": [] })); }
        else { eprintln!("{error}"); }
        return 1;
    }
    // A native job's module is built here too (LLP 3000.005): the schema's
    // Rust types written, cargo run, the module checked against the jobs.
    let native = match crate::native::build(&project, &bound) {
        Ok(built) => built,
        Err(problem) => {
            if options.json {
                println!("{}", json!({ "ok": false, "problems": [{ "code": problem.code, "message": problem.message, "rewrite": problem.rewrite, "guide": "native" }], "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": bound.notes, "acknowledgedNotes": [] }));
            } else {
                eprintln!("{problem}\n");
                eprintln!("check: 1 problem");
            }
            return 1;
        }
    };
    if let Err(error) = residency::remember(&project, &bound) { failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_STORE", format!("cannot write residency facts: {error}")); return 2; }
    let acquisition = if options.flags.contains_key("residency") {
        match residency::at_check(&project, &bound, options) {
            Ok(report) => report,
            Err(error) => { failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_INPUT", format!("residency measurement failed: {error}")); return 1; }
        }
    } else { None };
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#132-reviewed-notes — every failed check preserves review evidence, including independent policy refutations.
    let reviewed = if security.refuted() {
        note_reviews::Review { new: bound.notes.clone(), acknowledged: Vec::new() }
    } else { match note_reviews::review(&project, &compiled_sources, &bound.notes, &reviewable_notes, options.flags.contains_key("ack-notes")) {
        Ok(review) => review,
        Err(error) => {
            if options.json { println!("{}", json!({"ok": false, "problems": [{"code": "E_CHECK_NOTES", "message": error}], "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": bound.notes, "acknowledgedNotes": []})); }
            else { eprintln!("check: E_CHECK_NOTES: {error}"); }
            return 2;
        }
    } };
    let acknowledgment_not_applied = (security.refuted() && options.flags.contains_key("ack-security")).then_some(
        "--ack-security was not applied: a security invariant is refuted; an acknowledgment records specification rationale for a review note and cannot override a concrete counterexample. Repair the policy or the invariant and rerun check.");
    if !options.json { if let Some(message) = acknowledgment_not_applied { eprintln!("check: {message}"); } }
    let evidence=if options.json || options.flags.contains_key("review") || options.flags.contains_key("verbose") || options.flags.contains_key("ack-security") || security.notes.iter().any(|n|security_reviews::gated(&n.code)) {
        security::witnesses::collect(&project,&bound,&compiled_sources,&security.notes,options.json || options.flags.contains_key("review") || options.flags.contains_key("verbose") || options.flags.contains_key("ack-security"))
    } else { Vec::new() };
    let dispositions = match security_reviews::review_evidence(&project,&compiled_sources,&security.notes,
        if security.refuted(){None}else{options.flags.get("ack-security").map(|id|(id.as_str(),options.flags.get("reason").map(String::as_str).unwrap_or("")))},&evidence) {
        Ok(d)=>d,Err(error)=>{if options.json {println!("{}",json!({"ok":false,"security":&security,"problems":[{"code":"E_SECURITY_NOTES","message":error}]}));}else{eprintln!("check: E_SECURITY_NOTES: {error}");}return 2;}
    };
    check_surface::report(&project, &bound, options.json, Some((&security, &dispositions)));
    check_surface::remember(&project, &bound, Some((&security, &dispositions)));
    let security_failed=security.refuted()||security_reviews::pending(&dispositions);
    let visible_security=security.notes.iter().zip(&dispositions).filter(|(_,d)|!d.acknowledged).map(|(n,_)|n).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let compact_security=snapback4_lang::security::questionnaire_notes(&visible_security.iter().filter(|n|crate::note_rank::tier(&n.code)!=crate::note_rank::Tier::Review).map(|n|(*n).clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>());
    let present = |text: &str| crate::presentation::indexes(&bound.schema, text, options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
    let review = options.flags.contains_key("review") || options.flags.contains_key("verbose") || options.flags.contains_key("all");
    let details = review && (acquisition.is_none() || options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
    let certificates: Vec<_> = bound.programs.iter().map(|p| crate::scale::analyze_bound(&bound.schema, p)).collect();
    let scale_summary = crate::scale::summary(&certificates);
    let note_count = compact_security.len() + reviewed.new.len() + certificates.iter().map(|certificate| certificate.search.len()).sum::<usize>();
    let field_authority: Vec<_> = bound.schema.visible_tables().flat_map(|(name,table)| table.written_by.iter().map(move |(column,authority)|
        format!("{name}.{column}: written by .{}; visibility: {}", authority.owner, if authority.audience { "readable by audience" } else { "private to owner" }))).collect();
    let row_views = bound.schema.maintains.iter().filter_map(|m| m.row_key.as_ref().map(|k| json!({
        "view": m.target, "source": m.source, "sourceKey": k.source_key, "member": k.table,
        "memberKey": k.member_key, "fanout": k.fanout, "range": row_view_range(&bound.schema, m), "writerRecount": snapback4_core::engine::row_views::WRITER_RECOUNT, "recountChunk": snapback4_core::engine::row_views::RECOUNT_CHUNK
    }))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let elapsed = started.elapsed();
    let synced = bound.schema.synced().count();
    let sequences: Vec<_> = bound.schema.visible_tables().flat_map(|(name, table)| table.sequences.iter().map(move |(column, reference)| {
        format!("sequence {name}.{column}: counter per {} row through .{reference}; immutable, provisional while pending", table.columns[reference])
    })).collect();
    if options.json {
        println!("{}", json!({ "ok": !security_failed, "acknowledgmentNotApplied": acknowledgment_not_applied, "securityReviews": &dispositions, "security": &security, "followingHistory": bound.programs.iter().filter_map(|p| crate::scale::following::certificate(&bound.schema, &p.program)).collect::<Vec<_>>(), "rowViews": row_views, "fieldAuthority": field_authority, "sequences": sequences, "scaleSummary": scale_summary, "search": certificates.iter().flat_map(|certificate| &certificate.search).collect::<Vec<_>>(), "plans": if options.flags.contains_key("cost") { Some(bound.programs.iter().map(|p| (&p.program.name, p.selections())).collect::<BTreeMap<_, _>>()) } else { None }, "scale": if options.flags.contains_key("cost") { Some(&certificates) } else { None }, "tables": bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name, _)| bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_none()).count(), "views": bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name, _)| bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_some()).count(), "synced": synced, "operations": bound.programs.len(), "heldQueries": bound.schema.operations.iter().filter(|(_, fact)| fact.held).map(|(name, _)| name).collect::<Vec<_>>(), "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": reviewed.new, "acknowledgedNotes": reviewed.acknowledged, "predictionSummary": snapback4_lang::bind::residency::prediction_summary(&bound), "starterLeftovers": init::starter_leftovers_json(&project.root), "acquisition": acquisition, "residency": if options.flags.contains_key("residency") { Some(residency::facts(&bound)) } else { None }, "native": native.as_ref().map(|b| json!({ "module": b.path, "bytes": b.bytes, "jobs": b.jobs, "ms": b.ms })), "ms": elapsed.as_millis() }));
    } else {
        println!("{}",crate::presentation::check_verdict(security_failed,&table_counts(&bound.schema),synced,bound.programs.len(),if security.refuted(){"security invariant refuted"}else if security_failed{"security review required"}else{"compiled"},dispositions.iter().filter(|d|d.gated&&!d.acknowledged).count(),dispositions.iter().filter(|d|!d.gated&&!d.acknowledged&&crate::note_rank::tier(&d.code)==crate::note_rank::Tier::Review).count(),dispositions.iter().filter(|d|d.acknowledged).count(),note_count,elapsed.as_secs_f64()*1000.0,init::client_setup_hint(&project.root),&native.as_ref().map(|b|format!("; native: {} → {} ({} B, {} ms)",b.jobs.join(", "),b.path.display(),b.bytes,b.ms)).unwrap_or_default()));
        security::enforced(&security,&bound);
        if review && !reviewed.acknowledged.is_empty() { println!("{} reviewed notes (check --verbose to inspect); source edits require review again", reviewed.acknowledged.len()); }
        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
            for line in &sequences { println!("  {line}"); }
            for (name, _) in bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(_, t)| t.ephemeral.is_some()) {
                println!("  {name}: {}", snapback4_lang::bind::residency::table_line(&bound.schema, name));
            }
            for (name, fact) in bound.schema.operations.iter().filter(|(_, f)| f.ephemeral) {
                println!("{}", present(&snapback4_lang::bind::residency::operation_lines(name, fact)));
            }
        }
        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") { for line in &field_authority { println!("  {line}"); } }
        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
            // Verbose keeps every explanation, in the same consequence order as the summary.
            let mut ranked: Vec<&snapback4_lang::Diagnostic> = bound.notes.iter().collect();
            ranked.sort_by_key(|note| crate::note_rank::tier(&note.code));
            let ranked: Vec<snapback4_lang::Diagnostic> = ranked.into_iter().cloned().collect();
            for report in note_reports(&ranked, true) { println!("{}", present(&report)); }
        } else if review {
            // Notes are advisory: ranked and grouped after the ok line, repeats shortened (note_rank).
            let mut notes = reviewed.new.iter().chain(compact_security.iter()).map(|note| {
                let compact = completeness_note(note);
                crate::note_rank::Note { site: note.site.to_string(), code: note.code.clone(), summary: present(&note_summary(note)),
                    form: compact.is_none().then(|| crate::note_rank::compiled_form(&note.code, &note.rewrite)).flatten().map(|(lead, code)| (lead, present(&code))), compact }
            })
                .chain(certificates.iter().flat_map(|certificate| &certificate.search).map(|search| crate::note_rank::Note { site: search.site.to_string(), code: search.code.to_string(), summary: present(search.summary), form: None, compact: None }))
                .collect::<Vec<_>>();
            notes.sort_by_key(|note|(crate::note_rank::summary_order(&note.code),crate::note_rank::site_order(&note.site),note.code.clone(),note.summary.clone()));
            let total=notes.len();crate::note_rank::shortlist(&mut notes);
            let remaining=total-notes.len();
            if remaining>0 {println!("{remaining} more actionable notes in check --verbose/JSON");}
            for line in crate::note_rank::render(notes) { println!("{}", crate::presentation::abbreviate_quoted(&line)); }
        }
    }
    if !options.json { print!("{}",security_reviews::render_mode(&dispositions,&security.notes,review)); }
    if !options.json && (options.flags.contains_key("verbose") || options.flags.contains_key("cost")) {
        println!("{scale_summary}");
    }
    if !options.json && options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
        for search in certificates.iter().flat_map(|certificate| &certificate.search) {
            println!("  {} at {}: {}; rewrite: {} (guide {})", search.code, search.site, search.message, search.rewrite, search.guide);
        }
    }
    if details && !options.json {
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#17.7 — with every table online only, "never predicts" is every mutation and needs no action: verbose keeps it.
        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") || bound.schema.synced().next().is_some() { print_prediction(&bound, options.flags.contains_key("verbose")); }

 succeeded in 0ms:
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.
Git configuration change or unowned signal occurs.

## 2026-10-25 — G7.2 fix round 1: successor recovery and pipeline-aware fill

[The fix-round report](reports/20261025-g72-fix1/REPORT.md) resumes the preserved
changes on `069520dc23` after the prior disk-floor stop. A successor's own
staging failure now uses its predecessor's original terminal outcome to select
recovery. A committed predecessor leaves ordinary per-member recovery: valid A
survives B's deterministic 23505 or expired deadline, as at depth one. Only a
known predecessor abort takes the innocent whole-batch cascade rerun. Both
cascade sites now fall through to bounded solo attempts after that rerun's
second known failure. Unknown outcomes never authorize another attempt.

The four recovery witnesses fail before and pass after, including both cascade
sites and the mixed-deadline case; each checks durable receipts, contiguous
head and serial-equivalent state. The pipeline module now shares the SQLite
feature gate with program batching. Device and browser configurations fail
before with unresolved server-only imports and build afterward. Final core
and device builds without default features, native device, and browser wasm
all pass; wasm is 738,071 B gzip under the 738,490 B budget.

Postgres depth two drains available FIFO work and dispatches at the first empty
mailbox, within the existing target, 32-member cap and 1 ms collection bound.
It does not wait for predicted clients still held by the other writer. Depth
one and SQLite retain their coalescing policy, and idle arrivals never wait.
The policy witness fails before and passes after. RFC §8.1 documents the rule.

Two reversed-order loopback repetitions at 32 closed-loop clients record depth
1 → 2: W4 4,434 → 4,551 writes/s, 7.081 → 6.817 ms; W5-exact 4,531 → 4,803,
6.917 → 6.579 ms; W5-mix 4,602 → 4,811, 6.858 → 6.499 ms. Mean N falls from
30.48/30.48/31.22 to 15.24/15.42/15.33. Outstanding-batch dispatch histograms
(1, 2) are W4 (84, 0) → (4, 164), exact (84, 0) → (2, 164), mix
(82, 0) → (3, 164). All counters reconcile. Load is about 84 on 96 available
CPUs; these short local relative samples establish overlap, without an S1 or
general speedup claim.

Final full core: 1,278/0/480 (passed/failed/ignored); binary: 608/0/57;
focused G7.2: 33/0; core/HTTP RT0: 4/0 and 9/0. The complete Postgres script
records 847/2: only the on-wire-overlap and TCP_NODELAY witnesses explicitly
refuse socket-only qualification. The inherited evaluator 40001 does not occur.
Public serving passes, including release evolution, recovery, ownership, wire
parity and scale recounts. Both private clusters independently confirm status
3 with no pidfile. Final reference and PID audit results are in the report.
Raw evidence stays outside Git; no push, stash or Git configuration change occurs.

Final ref-check reports 569 LLPs and 11,984 references, zero errors or unchecked
references. All 8,732 continuation PID/birth instances, the prior run's 317,
and 537 public-script PID records are absent; the monitor has exited. PIDs left:
none. Minimum free disk is 123.83 GiB, final audit 128.77 GiB; the 30 GiB guard
never fires.

Mutation results, implicit final writes and explicit returned row objects are observers too. A query projection alone is not privacy: use column read rules to protect every operation. An authored projection excluding the column does not by itself disclose that value; filtering, ordering or copying it can. Audit JSON includes `column_audiences` (effective row rule plus private-owner gate). Unknown derived and opaque paths remain unproved.
